2. RECOGNITION 
 
A.   INTRODUCTION:    Student Representatives to the Board 
     
 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Introduce Sylvester, Avi, Patty and Dalia. 
  
Dalia Ibrahim, Athens High School 
  
Dalia Ibrahim is currently a senior at Athens High School.  She is the proud 
product of both Troy Union Elementary School and Larson Middle School.   
Throughout her time at Athens, Dalia has distinguished herself not only as an 
AP scholar, but also as the leader and voice of her peers.   She is currently 
serving in her fourth year as President of the Class of 2015, she is secretary of 
National Honor Society, and she will begin her fourth year as part of the girls ’ 
lacrosse program in the spring.   Dalia represents the very best Athens has to 
offer both inside and outside of the classroom.     
 
Avirath (Avi) Kumar, International Academy - East 
  
Avirath Kumar is currently a junior at IA -East and participates in a variety of 
activities.  Avi is a member of Model United Nations, IASA (The Indian 
American Student A ssociation), and DECA, the Competitive Marketing Club.  
Avi has also been the captain of the Athens men’s tennis team for the past 
two years. 
 
Sylvester Hanna, Niles Community High School 
  
Sylvester is a senior in his second year at N iles Community High S chool.  He 
participates in volunteer opportunities around the school including a recent 
clean-up project of painting and doing landscaping work.  In addition to taking 
classes and demonstrating quiet leadership skills at NCHS, Sylvester works 
full-time after school.  After graduation in June, th is Sterling Heights resident 
plans to attend college to pursue a career in law. 
 
Patty Durell, Troy High School 
  
This year we welcome back Patty Durrell, who is serving her third year as 
student representative to th e Board and is now a senior at Troy High School.  
Patty has participated in student government throughout her whole academic 
career and is now the President of Student Government.  She participates in 
Relay for Life  and is the captain of an exceptionally s uccessful team raising 
over $25,000 in the past three years.  Her favorite subject is English, and she 
hopes to find a future in New York.   
 
 
3
 
 
ALTERNATE BOARD REPS 
 
Bobby Hahn, Troy High School 
 
Bobby Hahn is a senior at Troy High School.  He is the Public Relations Officer 
for student government and is also on the Michigan Association of Student 
Council State Board.   As a member of the committee of Troy’s Relay for Life 
event, Bobby helps plan the 24 -hour cancer fundraiser while simultaneously 
participating in a suc cessful student run team.   With a love for business, he 
quickly achieved the position of secretary on the Business Professionals of 
America executive board. 
 
 
Zari Saynganthone, Niles Community High School 
 
Zari Saynganthone is a senior and a  core member of the N iles Community 
High School  Leadership and Social Justice Team, as well as the “Fingers 
Crossed” district theater troupe that performs and leads social justice 
discussions with 8th grade students around the district.  She is also a member  
of Women of Tomorrow.  In addition to attending classes and her participation 
in extracurricular activities at NCHS, Zari works part -time and attends Troy 
Adult Education night school to earn additional credits.  She is p lanning to 
attend Oakland Communit y College in the Dual Enrollment program in 
January.  Z ari will be preparing for a medical career in the U.S. Navy after 
graduation.

2. RECOGNITION 
 
       B.  2014–2015 National Achievement and National  
                                                      Merit Scholarship Semifinalists  
 
This year, more than 160,000 students, nationwide, entered the National Achievement 
Scholarship Program , which honors extremely high -achieving African-American  
students; however, only 1,600  are selected to be semifinalists.  Also t his year, more 
than one million students , nationwide, from some 22,000 high schools , entered the 
National Merit Scholarship Program; however, only 16,000  of these seniors are selected 
to be semifinalists.  This evening , we will be passing a resolution to c ommend and 
congratulate our one National Achievement Scholarship Semifinalist and our sixty-seven 
National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists. This is a remarkable number of semifinalists 
and a tribute to the staff and parents of the Troy School District .  Copies of this  
resolution will be presented to the students at a reception on October 15th. 
 
 
RECOMMENDATION:  Adopt the following resolution.

   SUPPORTED BY ________________________

Whereas, National Achievement students are the highest-scoring program participants 
in the states that constitute each region; and National Merit students reach semifinalist 
status by scoring in the top one and one -half percent on the National Preliminary 
Scholastic Aptitude Test (PSAT) and earning comparable scores on the Scholastic 
Aptitude Test (SAT).

this resolution in honor of the aforementioned Troy School District stud ents for their 
selection as 201 4-2015 National Achievement and National  Merit Scholarship 
Semifinalists.

2.  RECOGNITION            
 
C.  Superintendent’s Student Advisory Board 
 
The Superintendent’s Student Advisory Board is a new group  that was instituted 
this year for the purpose of providing action-oriented leadership opportunities to 
students at the high school level while also serving as a sounding -board to the 
Superintendent and Central Office administration.  Students will serve on this 
board throughout their high school caree r – graduating seniors will be replaced 
by a new group of freshmen at the beginning of each school year.  We received 
a total of 55 applications submitted by  outstanding students from Athens, 
International Academy-East, Niles and Troy High Schools. Out of these 
applications, 24 students were selected; 6 seniors, 7 juniors, 7 sophomores and 
4 freshmen.   Congratulations to the  following students who have been asked to 
serve as the Superintendent’s Advisory Board:      
 
 
Athens students: Alexzander Dudek, Curtis Hsu, Arsha Ali, Mara Quinn, 
Hannah Schell, Emily Skwarek, Goutham Karuppiah, and Katerina Levy 
 
 
Troy High students: Ben Mansky, Emma Thomas, Harsh Patel, Meghan Romig, 
Scott Mittelbrun, Afriti Chinoy, Esha Ghosalkar, and Keegan McInerney  
 
 
IA-East students: David Nguyen, Thejas Suvarna, Rida Farook, Peggy Randon, 
Shreya Shettigar, and Maheem Syed 
 
 
Niles students: Jeremy Huotari and Armondo Jalou

    SERVICES BUILDING  
    
   
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
President Philippart called the meeting to order at 7:00 p.m. 
 
In  addition  to  Dr.  Philippart,  present  were  Board  members 
Edmunds, Fleming, Miletti, and Underwood.    Mr. Hauff and Mr. 
Schmidt  were  absent.  Also  present  were  Superintendent 
Machesky;  administrators  Birmingham,  Jolly,  Keeling,  Dziatczak, 
Miller,  Mull,  Rajter,  Roncone,  and  Witt;  and  approximately  60 
additional
 members of the audience. 
 
RECOGNITION 
All‐State Athletes – 2014 Spring 
Sports 
 
 
 
  
 
 
 
 
 
Michigan Association of School 
Boards’ Awards: Board of 
Education, President Nancy 
Philippart and Secretary Karl 
Schmidt 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Mr.  Jolly  and  the  Board  recognized  the  following  students  who 
have received the distinction of  being
 names all‐state athletes in 
their  respective  sports:   Craig  Bazin  (Boys  Lacrosse),  Justice 
Bigelow  (Boys  Lacrosse),  Nicholas  Boynton  (Boys  Lacrosse),  Jay 
Darish  (Boys  Lacrosse),  Deepthi  Devireddy  (Girls  Tennis),  Jill 
Dougherty  (Girls  Lacrosse),  Kyle  Fietsam  (Boys  Lacrosse),  David 
Fracassa  (Boys  Lacrosse),  Amanda  Harris  (Girls  Soccer),  Sydney 
Heath  (Softball),  Lillian  Liang  (Girls  Tennis),  Ashley  Messina 
(Softball),  Sally  Muller  (Softball),  Jacob  Nelson  (Boys  Lacrosse), 
Kunai Tangri (Boys Track), and Sarah Troccoli (Girls Soccer). 
 
 
 
 
 
The Troy School Board received several honors this past year as a 
result of their commitment to excellence in governance. Tonight, 
the  Board  was  recognized  for  its  Honor  Board  achievement
  and 
its  receipt  of  a  Standard  of  Excellence  Award  by  the  Michigan 
Association  of  School  Boards.  Board  President  Nancy  Philippart 
was honored for her achievement of the  Master Board Member 
Award  and  Advocacy  Skill  Specialty  Award.   Karl  Schmidt  was 
recognized  for  achieving  MASB  Level  One  Certification  and  the 
Award  of  Merit.   All  of  these  honors  place  the  Troy  Board  of 
Education  as  being  among  a  handful  of  other  Michigan  school 
district  governing  bodies  that  have  demonstrated  a  true 
commitment  to  student  achievement  and  exemplary  leadership. 
The  Troy  community  is  fortunate  to  have  seven  Board  members 
who  are  so  committed  to  the  concept  of  life‐long  learning  and 
positive policy making.

Association of School Business 
Officials Certificate of Excellence

Boulan Park Middle School Service

ADDITIONS AND DELETIONS 
 
 
PUBLIC COMMUNICATION  
 
 
GENERAL ‐ CONSENT AGENDA 
14‐095 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
The  Association  of  School  Business  Officials  International’s 
Certificate of Excellence in
 Financial  Reporting Program honored 
the  Troy  School  District  for  excellence  in  the  preparation  and 
issuance of a high quality Comprehensive Annual Financial Report 
(CAFR) for the fiscal year ended June 30, 2013.  The Certificate of 
Excellence  in  Financial  Reporting  has  been  created  by  ASBO 
International  to  enable  business  officials  to  achieve  a  high 
standard  of  financial  reporting.  Mark  Rajter,  Assistant 
Superintendent  for  Business  Services,  and  his  hard‐working  staff 
were  recognized  for  all  their  effort  throughout  the  year  and  for 
their continued dedication to excellence. 
 
 
 
President Philippart introduced a new standing item during future 
Board  meetings  called  TSD  Mission  Moments.   The  Board  would 
like to highlight special things happening throughout the district. 
At  this  evening’s  Board  meeting,  Boulan  Park  Middle  School 
students  were  recognized  for  the  LINKS  program  and  their 
successful  project  to  collect  letters  and  items  for  our  troops  in 
Afghanistan.  The  LINKS  program  pairs  students  with  different 
learning  styles  and  fosters  an  environment  of  inclusion  and 
respect at Boulan Park. The troop’s project involved an ASD class 
and  their  8th  grade  mentors  from  the  LINKS  program.  The  group 
was led by Ms. Sandy Ford and Mr. Joe DeVault.   They collected 
1,022  personal  letters,  2,790  items  such  as  snacks,  bath  items, 
gloves,  CD’s,  games  and  cash  totaling  $142.  The  items  were 
organized  into  care  packages  and  sent  to  troops  overseas.   The 
Board  congratulated  the  students  for  their  efforts  in  this 
important project.   
 
 
None. 
 
 
None. 
 
   
It was moved by Mrs. Fleming and supported by Ms. Underwood, 
that  BE  IT  THEREFORE  RESOLVED  that  the  Troy  Board  of 
Education takes action to approve item A on the consent agenda.

Schmidt:  Absent  
 
 
 
Mr.  Witt,  Assistant  Superintendent,  Human  Resources,  shared  a 
brief  update  on  teacher  staffing  for 
the  2014/2015  school  year.  
He reviewed the primary factors which influenced the number of 
teachers hired, which included retirements/resignations, full‐year 
leaves of absence, FTE status, and student enrollment.  He shared 
an overview of the interview selection process and indicated that 
44  new  teachers  were  hired  this  year.   He  added  that  eight  of 
them  are  TSD  graduates  and  that  27  have  an  educational 
background  beyond a Bachelor’s Degree.   He also indicated  that 
the hiring process was almost complete and that only a couple of 
positions needed to be filled.   
 
Dr. Philippart inquired about the hiring process and if it is possible 
for one or two individuals to make independent hiring decisions.  
Mr. Witt provided a background of how the current hiring process 
was  developed  and  indicated  that  a  group  of  3  or  4  principals 
14
 4 September 2, 2014 
 
 
 
 
initially  screen  candidates  then  collectively  identify  applicants 
based on certain factors to move on to the next level.   He stated 
that  through  this  process,  he  is  confident  that  only  the  best 
candidates succeed into the candidate pool. 
 
 
CURRICULUM 
Student Learning Showcase 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Summer Learning Update/Summer 
Success Academy
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Elementary and Secondary 
Enrollment Update 
 
 
 
Mrs. Anne Mull, Director of Instruction and Assessment provided 
a  PowerPoint  presentation  reviewing  the  history  of  the  Student 
Learning Showcase and the changes that have occurred over the 
years.  She indicated that the Showcase is always held in May and 
that
  two years ago it became open to the  community.   She also 
indicated  that  presentations  no  longer  have  to  be  displayed  on 
boards  but  can  be  shown  on  iPads,  computers,  projectors  and 
TVs.   She added that teams from each school will be attending a 
School Improvement Roll‐Out session in September to discuss the 
future  of  the  Student  Learning  Showcase  such  as  what  can  be 
done better or different and when to schedule the next one.  She 
asked that any feedback the Board might have be sent to Central 
Office.   
 
Dr. Philippart asked if the county still assists with the Showcase.  
Mrs.
 Mull indicated that Oakland Schools cut the funding for the 
program in 2011 and no longer participates.   
 
 
 
Mr. Mike Jolly, Director of Athletics, CTE & Enrichment, shared a 
video  of  the  summer  learning  program  offered  in  the  District.  
The video provided an overview of the summer school program, 
youth  services
  program,  the  TOTS  program,  CARE  Company 
summer  program,  enrichment  offerings  for  elementary,  middle 
and high school students, swim classes, adult education and  ESL 
programs.   Mr.  Jolly  shared  that  the  programs  offered  a  diverse 
educational opportunity for students to earn or recover credits in 
math, science, literacy, over 3200 registrants were enrolled over 
the  summer  and  125  TSD  staff  members  worked  within  the 
program.   
 
Ms.  Underwood  inquired  about  the  difference  in  enrollment 
between Athens and Troy High and asked if students and parents 
have  been  polled  as  to  why  they  are  not  enrolling.   Mr.  Jolly 
indicated  that  they  are  looking  into  doing
  a  poll  to  address  the 
issue.  Dr. Philippart asked if we have been able to accommodate 
all  students  who  are  interested.   Mr.  Jolly  stated  that  more 
teachers would be hired according to enrollment.     
 
 
Mark Dziatczak, Assistant Superintendent, Secondary Instruction, 
and  Jan  Keeling,  Assistant  Superintendent,  Elementary 
Instruction,  presented  a  report  to  the  Board  on  the  District’s

Ron Curtis, Senior Project Manager from Barton Malow, provided 
the  Board  with  an  update  on  the  status  of  bond  construction 
projects going on throughout the District.  
He shared photographs 
that  were  taken  to  show  the  progress  that  has  been  made  to 
date.   There  was  discussion  with  the  Board  on  the  dates  of 
completion for front offices and various other projects.   
 
 
Dr. Machesky shared a video with the Board that had been shown 
to District staff at an
 opening day event at Athens on August 26th.  
Staff from all employee groups ‐  teachers, secretaries, para pros, 
EnviroClean,  Sodexo,  and  transportation  were  in  attendance.  He 
explained  that  to  kick  off  the  school  year,  he  introduced  the 
concept of OneTroy, which represents our renewed commitment 
to  come  together  as  one  unified  school  district  that  values  the 
role  every  employee  plays  in  our  collective  focus  of  education 
students.    He  wanted  to  provide  an  opportunity  to  challenge 
staff as they return for the new school year to be creative, take a 
chance, and dare to fail.    
 
Dr.  Machesky  also  mentioned  that  our  new  central  enrollment 
process  has  been  going  great  and  mentioned  the  Central 
Enrollment team who has enrolled hundreds of families greeting 
everyone  with  smiles,  professionalism  and  patience.  He 
commented on the custodial crews who have been working hard 
to  get  the  buildings  ready  for  school  during  major  construction.  
He also commented on the Summer Learning team who worked 
throughout  the  summer  impacting  thousands  of  students.   He 
also talked about the first day of school today and that students 
were excited about the new school year.  He commented that he 
visited several buildings today and plans to stop by each building 
by the end of the week.   
 
Dr. Philippart asked if buses were late due to the construction on 
Northfield  Parkway.   Dr.  Machesky  commented  that  during  the 
first  week  or  two  of  school  it  is  common  for  buses  to  be  a  few 
minutes late.   
 
Ms.  Underwood  welcomed  everyone  back  to  the  new  school 
year.     
 
Mrs.  Fleming  is  excited  to  be 
a  small  part  of  OneTroy  and 
congratulated  Dr.  Philippart  and  Mr.  Schmidt  for  their  MASB 
18
 8 September 2, 2014

awards and recognition.  
 
Mrs. Edmunds commented that she was in attendance at Athens 
on  August  26
th  and  thought  the  new  school  year  looks  very 
promising.   She  also  mentioned  that  Tim  Currier  from  Beier 
Howlett was named “Lawyer of the Year” in the Oakland Press.   
 
Dr. Philippart announced that it is a great start to the school year 
and that the Board would be meeting next on

C.   REPORT:  Teacher/Administrator Evaluation Summary 
 
At the September 16, 2014, Board of Education Workshop, Board 
members had the opportunity to review a summary the 2013/2014 
Teacher/Administrator Evaluation process, which was provided by 
Jasen Witt, Assistant Superintendent, Human Resources.  Mr. Witt 
will now share his presentation with the community.   
 
 
 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report is presented by 
 Jasen Witt, Assistant Superintendent, Human Resources. 
 
112
October 7, 2014
Jasen Witt
113
 3rd year of the Troy Educator Appraisal Model 
(TEAM).
 TEAM has undergone modification each year.
 Most significant change for 2013/14 was the 
movement to evaluation at the component level.
 We also moved from STAGES to Teachscape for 
management of evaluations.

for TSD administrators (Principals, Assistant

Leadership Performance Matrix.
 Goals-based aspects are included.

 Work in Progress for 2014/15:
 Move towards goal-oriented evaluation process for 
District-Wide Administrators and for Non-
Instructional Administrators.
 These administrators will, in turn, work within their 
respective departments on established departmental 
goals.
120
 Michigan Public School Districts are called to 
have a performance-based compensation (PBC) 
model covering teachers.
 Initial Year for TSD PBC model was the 
2013/2014 school year.
 Eligibility for PBC is tied to outcome of TEAM 
evaluation, special training, & significant 
contributions.
121
 The TSD PBC model also includes an 
attendance component.
 Total Absences 2012/2013 for Personal-Family 
Illness/Bereavement or Personal Business – 7,790
 Total Absences 2013/2014 for Personal-Family 
Illness/Bereavement or Personal Business – 6,112
Reduction of 1,678 Absences

Portion of PBC – 416

 Revisions to PBC Model for 2014/2015
1. Streamlined process;
2. Further aligned PBC model with 
TEAM; and
3. Attendance component revised to 
exclude up to 4 annual absence 
days for bereavement of a relative from 
absence totals.

7. CURRICULUM 
 
C.   REPORT:  Advanced Placement Scholars 
 
On an annual basis the number and percentage of students 
participating in the high school advanced placement programs are 
reviewed. Additionally, we track the annual Equity and Access scores 
as they help further clarify the number of graduates taking 
advantage of advanced plac ement classes as well as scoring three 
or higher on the exam.  
  
Our annual goal is to increase the number and representatio n of 
students taking AP courses and scoring a three or higher.  The 
following is a report that identifies the number of students receiving 
various AP honors as well as a historical breakdown of courses and  
test-taking along with 2014 AP and Equity scores.  
 
 
 
 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report is presented by 
 Mark Dziatczak, Assistant Superintendent, Secondary Instruction.

A.  CONSIDERATION:  FOOD SERVICES WIRELESS TEMPERATURE 
MONITORING SYSTEM EQUIPMENT AND 
INSTALLATION 
 
Invitations to bid were sent to 5 potential bidders with 2 responding (none of 
which were “no bid”).  Bid responses were reviewed by  Mr. Todd Hensley, 
Purchasing Supervisor and Ms. Gayle Moran, Sodexo Manager, and it is 
recommended the bid be awarded as stated below.  
 
 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 
 Moved by:   
 
 Supported by: 
 
 Be it therefore resolved  that the bid for food services wireless temperature 
monitoring system equipment and installation be awarded to SMART Temp, LLC 
in the amount of $ 38,504.00 and that th is amount be approved as an 
expenditure of the 2014-2015 Food Services Fund. 
 
 
 
 
 Yes: 
 
 No: 
151
  
 
 
TO:       Rich Machesky, Superintendent  
 
FROM:  Todd Hensley, Purchasing Supervisor   
           
RE:       BID 9796  - Food Service Wireless Temperature 
            Monitoring System Equipment and Installation 
 
DATE:   October 1, 2014  
 
 
 
Specification to furnish and install a wireless web-based monitoring system , which 
has the ability to remotely and accurately measure real -time operational 
temperature data from freezers, coolers, milk  boxes, storerooms, and dishwashers 
through the use of temperature sensors, was compiled.  Invitations to bid were sent 
to five potential bidders with two responding. 
 
After analyzing the inform ation provided in the proposals  and reviewing the 
proposals with Gayle Moran, Director/General Manager, Sodexo Food Services, I am 
recommending that SMART Temp, LLC, be awarded the total bid for an am ount of 
$38,504.00.  This selection is the 2nd quoted SMART item (see attached tab) and is 
their 2014 upgraded Food Monitoring System software.  This information and 
selection was also submitted to the Michigan Department of Education (MDE) for 
approval, which is attached for your review .  This expense for equipment and 
installation will be taken from the 2014-15 Food Service Fund.  
  
If you have any questions, please let me know.  
 
 
 
 
Cc:   Pat Orczykowski – Business Department  
 Purchasing Department – File

C.  CONSIDERATION:  ATHENS HIGH SCHOOL ADDITION AND 
REMODELING MASONRY WORK 
 
Bid responses for masonry work for the Athens High School addition 
and remodeling project were reviewed by Mr. Todd Hensley, 
Purchasing Supervisor, and Mr. Ronald Curtis, Senior Project Manager, 
Barton Malow, and it is recommended the bid be awarded as stated 
below.  
 
 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 
 Moved by:   
 
 Supported by: 
 
 Be it therefore resolved  that the bid for masonry work for the Athens 
High School addition and remodeling project be awarded to Efficient 
Design, Inc. in the amount of $73,482.00,  and that this amount be 
approved as an expenditure of the 2014 Capital Projects Fund. 
 
 
 
 Yes: 
 
 No: 
157
  
 
 
TO:  Rich Machesky - Superintendent  
 
FROM:  Todd Hensley – Purchasing Supervisor   
           
RE:  Bid Package 4  - Athens High School - Masonry  
 
DATE:  October 6, 2014 
 
 
 
 
This is a re -bid of the masonry category for the Athens High School Addition and 
Remodeling Bond Project – 2013 Bond Program, Series 1.  After analyzing the 
information provided in the proposals and reviewing the proposals with Barton 
Malow, I concur with Barton Malow ’s recommendation (see attached documents) of 
awarding the following company the masonry work: 
 
 
 
The awarded amount totaling $73,482, is approximately $3,482 over the budgeted 
amount of $70,000, creating an unfavorable budget variance.  
 
If you have any questions, let me know.  
 
 
 
 
Cc:   Pat Orczykowski – Business Department

October 2, 2014 
 
Mr. Kenneth Miller, Executive Director 
Troy School District 
1140 Rankin 
Troy, MI  48083 
 
Re: 2013 Bond Program, Series 1 
 Bid Package 4:  Athens High School Addition and Re modeling 
 Contract Award Recommendation for Masonry 
 
Dear Mr. Miller: 
 
Contracts were awarded on August 5, 2014 for Bid Package 4:  Athens High School.  The work of this 
package is to renovate the old shop area into Large Group Instruction, and to convert the photo studio into 
shared classroom space.  There were no bids received at that time for masonry work, and a budget of 
$70,000 was set aside to complete this category.  We subsequently put the package out and received three 
bids on September 18, 2014.  A tabulation of these bids is attached. 
 
We reviewed the proposal and qualifications of apparent low bidder, and recommend a contract be 
awarded as follows: 
 
Category   Contractor    Amount 
 
 042000 Masonry  Efficient Design, Inc.   $73,482. 
 
 
 
Please contact me should you have any questions. 
 
 
Sincerely, 
 
Senior Project Manager 
 
Attachments:  Bid Tabulation 
C:  Ms. Nichol Bruno - BMC

10.  OTHER            
 
B.  2014/2015 Superintendent Annual Report & Goals 
 
The Troy School District had another successful year in 201 3/2014. The 
excellence of our staff and students continues to be recognized at national 
and state levels and we are committed to making necessary 
improvements to ensure the success of every student. The following 
presentation will provide a brief overview of our  Annual Report and 
Superintendent Goals for this school year.   
 
 
         
 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: 
 
This report will be given by Dr. Machesky. 
 
163
October 7, 2014
164
Mission Statement
The	purpose	of	the	Troy	School	District	
is	to	ensure	learning	for	all	members	of	
the	school	community.
165
State Accountability System
Schools	and	districts	are	expected	to	have	85%	of	all	
students	and	student	groups	proficient	on	state	
assessment	by	2021‐2022
 Student	groups	are	defined	as	any	group	of	30	or	
more	students	who	share	economic,	ethnic,	
limited	English	proficient,	students	with	
disabilities,	or	other	characteristics.
 Schools	and	districts	have	been	assigned	by	the	
state	an	individual	yearly	Annual	Measureable	
Objective	(AMO)
 AMO	was	calculated	using	the	proficiency	rate
from the	2011‐2012 school	year	to	create	annual	
targets	that	increment	to	an	end	proficiency	rate	
of	85%	by	2021‐2022

Operational Effectiveness/Responsiveness
Business	Services
 Institute	operational	efficiency	and	accuracy	as	
measured	by	an	annual	stakeholder	survey
Strategies:	
 Institute	customer	service	metrics	to	include	
visibility	and	responsiveness;	and
 Implement	100% usage	compliance	of	e‐finance	
modules	including	HR,	Payroll,	and	Accounts	
Receivable.

177
Operational Effectiveness/Responsiveness
Human	Resources
 Institute	operational	efficiency	and	accuracy	as	
measured	by	an	annual	stakeholder	survey	
Strategies:	
 Institute	customer	service	metrics	to	include	visibility	
and	responsiveness;	and
 Update,	develop	and	communicate	important	
informational	links	for	employees	including	links	to	
personnel	policies	and	procedures,	certification	
information,	collective	bargaining	agreements,	benefit	
information,	laws	impacting	employees,	and	human	
resources	department	contacts.

Instruction - Achievement
College	&	Career	Readiness
 85%	Successful	completion	of	early	college	and	career	
programs	of	study
Strategies:	
 Monitor	programs	that	lead	to	post‐secondary	credit	or	
certification	and	promote	annual	growth	and	attainment.
 Maximize	achievement	gains	for	students	identified	within	any	
sub‐group	as	reported	on	a	monthly	basis
Strategies:	
 Identify		at‐risk	factors	that	cause	students	to	be	identified	as	
part	of	the	bottom	30%;	and
 Implement	and	monitor	changes	that	target	specific	at‐risk	
factors.

Strategies:

 Articulate	cohesion	between	district	and	building	goals.
